d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eLarge Language Model (LLM) coding agents process tens of thousands of tokens per session through tool outputs — shell commands, file reads, test results, and build logs. These outputs enter the context window raw, consuming budget that could serve reasoning. We present \u003cstrong\u003esqz\u003c/strong\u003e, a transparent compression layer that operates \u003cem\u003ebefore\u003c/em\u003e tool outputs are injected into context. sqz combines domain-specific structural formatters, content-addressed deduplication, adaptive pressure-aware compression, and entropy-based safety routing to achieve a mean 24.7% reduction on first-pass content and up to 92% savings on repeated reads — without requiring model fine-tuning, prompt modification, or changes to the agent's workflow. Measured across 3,003 real compressions in production agentic sessions, sqz saved 178,442 tokens while maintaining full semantic fidelity on downstream tasks.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003chr\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e1. Introduction\u003c/h2\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-1-introduction\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: 1. Introduction\" href=\"#1-introduction\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThe emergence of agentic coding tools — Claude Code, Cursor, Codex, Kiro, Gemini CLI — has shifted LLM usage from single-turn question-answering to multi-turn, tool-augmented sessions that can span thousands of interactions. In these sessions, the dominant source of context consumption is not user prompts or model responses, but \u003cstrong\u003etool outputs\u003c/strong\u003e: the results of shell commands, file reads, and API calls that accumulate in the context window over time.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eRecent measurements show that average agent session length tripled from under 2,000 tokens in late 2023 to over 5,400 tokens by late 2025, with the bulk of that growth coming from tool results accumulating in context [1]. This creates a triple penalty: higher costs, increased latency from longer prefills, and degraded reasoning quality as attention must span an ever-growing context.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003ePrior work on context compression has focused on two regimes: (1) \u003cstrong\u003eprompt compression\u003c/strong\u003e that removes redundant tokens from instructions before inference [2, 3, 4], and (2) \u003cstrong\u003epost-hoc context pruning\u003c/strong\u003e that evicts stale conversation history after it enters the window [5, 6]. Both operate on text \u003cem\u003ealready inside\u003c/em\u003e the context. sqz addresses a third, underexplored regime: \u003cstrong\u003epre-injection compression of tool outputs\u003c/strong\u003e — reducing token count before content ever enters the LLM's context window.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThis distinction is architecturally significant. Pre-injection compression:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cul dir=\"auto\"\u003e\n\u003cli\u003ePreserves prompt cache validity (the message prefix never changes)\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eOperates independently of the LLM provider\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eRequires no model cooperation or fine-tuning\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eWorks transparently across all agent frameworks\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003chr\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e2. System Architecture\u003c/h2\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-2-system-architecture\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: 2. System Architecture\" href=\"#2-system-architecture\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp align=\"center\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\n  \u003ca target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noopener noreferrer\" href=\"/ojuschugh1/sqz/blob/main/assets/sqz-architecture.png\"\u003e\u003cimg src=\"/ojuschugh1/sqz/raw/main/assets/sqz-architecture.png\" alt=\"sqz system architecture\" width=\"700\" style=\"max-width: 100%;\"\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\n  \u003cbr\u003e\n  \u003cem\u003eFigure 1: sqz system architecture — integration surfaces, 8-stage compression pipeline, and supporting subsystems.\u003c/em\u003e\n\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003esqz operates as a PreToolUse hook — a command that the agent framework invokes before delivering tool output to the model. The system comprises four integration surfaces (CLI Hook, MCP Server, Browser Extension, IDE Extension) feeding into a unified Rust core (\u003ccode\u003esqz_engine\u003c/code\u003e) with an 8-stage compression pipeline. Three supporting subsystems handle persistence (SHA-256 cache, SQLite FTS5 store, CTX session graph), intelligence (TOON lossless encoder, AST parser supporting 18 languages, model router), and analytics (USD cost tracking, multi-agent budgets, correction log). The entire system operates with zero telemetry, fully offline, and is air-gap capable.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThe architecture has five logical stages:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e2.1 Domain-Specific Structural Formatters\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-21-domain-specific-structural-formatters\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: 2.1 Domain-Specific Structural Formatters\" href=\"#21-domain-specific-structural-formatters\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eRather than treating all text uniformly (as token-level compression methods do), sqz recognizes 90+ CLI output patterns and applies format-aware transformations:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cul dir=\"auto\"\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eTest output\u003c/strong\u003e → failures only (passing tests stripped)\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eGit status\u003c/strong\u003e → compact change summary\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eJSON responses\u003c/strong\u003e → null-stripped, key-projected, array-collapsed\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eDocker/kubectl\u003c/strong\u003e → name/image/status table extraction\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eBuild logs\u003c/strong\u003e → error-focused summarization\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThis exploits the observation from CodeComp [7] that source code and structured outputs have formal semantics governed by structure rather than surface-level co-occurrence. A \u003ccode\u003ecargo test\u003c/code\u003e output of 500 lines can be faithfully represented by 30 lines containing only the failures and their locations.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e2.2 Content-Addressed Deduplication\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-22-content-addressed-deduplication\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: 2.2 Content-Addressed Deduplication\" href=\"#22-content-addressed-deduplication\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eAgentic sessions exhibit high repetition: the same file is read multiple times, the same command re-run after edits, the same API response fetched across turns. sqz maintains a persistent SHA-256 content cache (surviving across process invocations via SQLite) and replaces repeated content with a 13-token reference marker (\u003ccode\u003e§ref:HASH§\u003c/code\u003e).\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThis is analogous to the demand paging concept described in [8], where context content is loaded on-demand rather than kept resident. The key insight is that in coding sessions, \u003cstrong\u003ethe dominant savings come not from compressing individual outputs harder, but from recognizing that the same content appears repeatedly.\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eA reference is only emitted while the content it points at is plausibly still in the model's context: refs expire after a freshness window (30 minutes of wall-clock time by default) and are invalidated eagerly when the agent framework compacts its history, at which point the content is re-sent in full. As of 1.4.0, entries can also be \u003cstrong\u003epinned\u003c/strong\u003e (\u003ccode\u003esqz pin\u003c/code\u003e), exempting them from the freshness check — useful for stable knowledge-base content (project docs, system prompts) that multiple agents sharing the session store reference across days.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eNear-duplicate deltas.\u003c/strong\u003e A file re-read after a small edit is not byte-identical, so exact hashing misses it. sqz detects near-duplicates of cached entries and emits a line-level delta — only the changed lines, plus a reference to the version the model already has. Since 1.5.0 a session-lifetime MinHash LSH index selects delta candidates, so a file re-read late in a long session still matches its earlier version rather than only the most recent cache entries.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eRanged (slice) references, 1.8.0.\u003c/strong\u003e A field measurement over one user's real Claude Code sessions (measurement script published [15]; counters reset at each compaction boundary, edit/write confirmations excluded) found that byte-identical repeats were rare (0.3% of result characters; 1 in 542 file reads) but 8.4% of file reads were a \u003cem\u003eline range\u003c/em\u003e of a file already read in full — \u003ccode\u003esed -n '41,80p'\u003c/code\u003e after \u003ccode\u003ecat\u003c/code\u003e, or a ranged read tool call. Exact hashing misses these entirely, and near-duplicate matching does too: a 40-line slice of a 500-line file has a Jaccard similarity near 0.1. sqz therefore checks \u003cem\u003econtainment\u003c/em\u003e: when new output is, on line boundaries, a slice of a fresh full read whose served bytes also contain the slice, it emits \u003ccode\u003e§ref:HASH:L41-80§\u003c/code\u003e instead of the lines. The second condition matters — the slice must appear in what was actually delivered to the model, so a ranged reference can never point at lines a lossy stage dropped. Guards: at least 160 bytes and two lines, and the same freshness and compaction rules as whole-content refs. The reference is not a summary; it is a pointer to bytes the model already holds, and \u003ccode\u003esqz expand 'HASH:L41-80'\u003c/code\u003e recovers exactly those lines on demand.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eMeasured empirically: dedup accounts for 92% reduction on repeat reads, compared to 21-58% from first-pass structural compression.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e2.3 Adaptive Pressure-Aware Compression\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-23-adaptive-pressure-aware-compression\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: 2.3 Adaptive Pressure-Aware Compression\" href=\"#23-adaptive-pressure-aware-compression\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eInspired by research on adaptive compression rate allocation [9, 10], sqz dynamically adjusts compression intensity based on session pressure — the rate of token injection over a sliding window:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cul dir=\"auto\"\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eLow pressure\u003c/strong\u003e (\u0026lt;50% of estimated budget consumed in 30 minutes): Default mode, preserve maximum detail\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eHigh pressure\u003c/strong\u003e (\u0026gt;80%): Escalate to aggressive compression, accepting higher information loss\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eCritical\u003c/strong\u003e (\u0026gt;90%): Maximum compression + proactive dedup\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThis implements the core finding from ACC-RAG [10] that static compression rates are suboptimal: early in a session detail preservation matters more, while late in a session token scarcity demands harder compression.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e2.4 Entropy-Based Safety Routing\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-24-entropy-based-safety-routing\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: 2.4 Entropy-Based Safety Routing\" href=\"#24-entropy-based-safety-routing\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eNot all content should be compressed. Stack traces contain exact line numbers that compression could corrupt. Configuration files with credentials should not be parsed and reformatted. Migration SQL must be preserved byte-exact.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003esqz uses Shannon entropy analysis combined with pattern detection to classify content into risk tiers:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cul dir=\"auto\"\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eSafe mode\u003c/strong\u003e (0% compression): Stack traces, private keys, credentials, database migrations\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eDefault mode\u003c/strong\u003e (20-40% compression): Normal code, git output, build logs\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eAggressive mode\u003c/strong\u003e (40-60% compression): Repetitive logs, verbose JSON, boilerplate\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThis addresses the concern raised in [11] that compression methods which rely on generic importance metrics can inadvertently remove functionally critical tokens.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eVersion 1.4.0 extended the safety model with three fidelity guarantees derived from field reports:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cul dir=\"auto\"\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eByte-faithful file reads.\u003c/strong\u003e The MCP file tools (\u003ccode\u003esqz_read_file\u003c/code\u003e, \u003ccode\u003esqz_grep\u003c/code\u003e, \u003ccode\u003esqz_list_dir\u003c/code\u003e) bypass every content-altering pipeline stage — a file read returns its bytes verbatim apart from ANSI stripping. Token savings on this path come exclusively from dedup references on repeat reads, never from dropping content. (Previously, entropy truncation could silently remove below-median-entropy segments from source files.)\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eRefuse-to-guess formatting.\u003c/strong\u003e Structural formatters bail out to raw passthrough rather than emit a partial or misclassified summary. The \u003ccode\u003egit status\u003c/code\u003e formatter, for example, parses porcelain output strictly, matches localized long-format section headers against git's own translation files (it/de/fr/es/pt_PT), and passes any unrecognized locale or section through unmodified — a wrong summary is strictly worse than no compression.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eUTF-8 boundary safety.\u003c/strong\u003e All truncation and splitting of user-controlled text rounds to character boundaries, so densely non-ASCII output (Cyrillic, CJK, Arabic, emoji) can never crash the compressor mid-pipeline; the property is enforced by randomized tests over arbitrary strings and cut points.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e2.5 N-gram Abbreviation\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-25-n-gram-abbreviation\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: 2.5 N-gram Abbreviation\" href=\"#25-n-gram-abbreviation\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eFor session-level compression beyond dedup, sqz observes recurring multi-token phrases across the session and introduces compact abbreviations. This technique is inspired by BPE (Byte Pair Encoding) applied at the output level rather than the tokenizer level — frequently co-occurring phrases are replaced with shorter representations.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eAbbreviation is deliberately \u003cstrong\u003elossy for identifiers\u003c/strong\u003e: only the first occurrence of a repeated phrase survives verbatim, so a commit SHA or file path embedded in a repeated phrase would be replaced by its abbreviation symbol on later lines. Since 1.4.0 the technique is opt-out (\u003ccode\u003e--no-abbrev\u003c/code\u003e or \u003ccode\u003eSQZ_NO_ABBREV=1\u003c/code\u003e, mirroring the dedup opt-out) and the generated agent guidance documents the escape hatch, so an agent that needs to copy-paste identifiers verbatim can disable it per command.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003chr\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e3. Integration Model\u003c/h2\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-3-integration-model\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: 3. Integration Model\" href=\"#3-integration-model\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eUnlike prompt compression tools that require API interception or model-specific adapters, sqz integrates at the \u003cstrong\u003etool execution boundary\u003c/strong\u003e — a universal hook point that exists in every agent framework:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cmarkdown-accessiblity-table\u003e\u003ctable\u003e\n\u003cthead\u003e\n\u003ctr\u003e\n\u003cth\u003eFramework\u003c/th\u003e\n\u003cth\u003eHook Mechanism\u003c/th\u003e\n\u003c/tr\u003e\n\u003c/thead\u003e\n\u003ctbody\u003e\n\u003ctr\u003e\n\u003ctd\u003eClaude Code\u003c/td\u003e\n\u003ctd\u003ePreToolUse JSON hook (Bash and PowerShell tools)\u003c/td\u003e\n\u003c/tr\u003e\n\u003ctr\u003e\n\u003ctd\u003eCursor\u003c/td\u003e\n\u003ctd\u003e.cursor/rules/*.mdc\u003c/td\u003e\n\u003c/tr\u003e\n\u003ctr\u003e\n\u003ctd\u003eWindsurf\u003c/td\u003e\n\u003ctd\u003e.windsurfrules guidance\u003c/td\u003e\n\u003c/tr\u003e\n\u003ctr\u003e\n\u003ctd\u003eCline / Roo Code\u003c/td\u003e\n\u003ctd\u003e.clinerules guidance\u003c/td\u003e\n\u003c/tr\u003e\n\u003ctr\u003e\n\u003ctd\u003eKiro\u003c/td\u003e\n\u003ctd\u003e.kiro/hooks/ JSON schema\u003c/td\u003e\n\u003c/tr\u003e\n\u003ctr\u003e\n\u003ctd\u003eGemini CLI\u003c/td\u003e\n\u003ctd\u003eBeforeTool hook\u003c/td\u003e\n\u003c/tr\u003e\n\u003ctr\u003e\n\u003ctd\u003eOpenCode\u003c/td\u003e\n\u003ctd\u003eTypeScript plugin\u003c/td\u003e\n\u003c/tr\u003e\n\u003ctr\u003e\n\u003ctd\u003eCodex\u003c/td\u003e\n\u003ctd\u003eAGENTS.md guidance + config.toml MCP entry\u003c/td\u003e\n\u003c/tr\u003e\n\u003ctr\u003e\n\u003ctd\u003eZed\u003c/td\u003e\n\u003ctd\u003e.rules/AGENTS.md instructions + context_servers MCP entry\u003c/td\u003e\n\u003c/tr\u003e\n\u003ctr\u003e\n\u003ctd\u003eCopilot CLI\u003c/td\u003e\n\u003ctd\u003epreToolUse hook\u003c/td\u003e\n\u003c/tr\u003e\n\u003ctr\u003e\n\u003ctd\u003eAny MCP server\u003c/td\u003e\n\u003ctd\u003e\u003ccode\u003esqz-mcp proxy\u003c/code\u003e wrapper (since 1.6.0)\u003c/td\u003e\n\u003c/tr\u003e\n\u003c/tbody\u003e\n\u003c/table\u003e\u003c/markdown-accessiblity-table\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eA single \u003ccode\u003esqz init\u003c/code\u003e command detects installed frameworks and configures the appropriate hooks. The compression is transparent — the agent receives compressed output without knowing sqz exists.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eSince 1.6.0 the MCP proxy provides a framework-independent integration surface: \u003ccode\u003esqz-mcp proxy -- \u0026lt;upstream command\u0026gt;\u003c/code\u003e wraps any stdio MCP server, compresses its tool results through the full pipeline (dedup references on repeats, safe mode for stack traces and secrets, error results untouched), optionally compacts verbose tool descriptions in \u003ccode\u003etools/list\u003c/code\u003e, and injects an \u003ccode\u003esqz_expand\u003c/code\u003e tool so the agent can recover any original byte-exact. The client sees a normal MCP server, so this works with every MCP client regardless of whether it supports hooks.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003chr\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e4. Evaluation\u003c/h2\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-4-evaluation\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: 4. saved\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/td\u003e\n\u003ctd\u003e\u003cstrong\u003e178,442\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/td\u003e\n\u003c/tr\u003e\n\u003ctr\u003e\n\u003ctd\u003eAverage reduction\u003c/td\u003e\n\u003ctd\u003e24.7%\u003c/td\u003e\n\u003c/tr\u003e\n\u003ctr\u003e\n\u003ctd\u003ePeak reduction (dedup)\u003c/td\u003e\n\u003ctd\u003e92%\u003c/td\u003e\n\u003c/tr\u003e\n\u003c/tbody\u003e\n\u003c/table\u003e\u003c/markdown-accessiblity-table\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" (safe mode)\u003c/td\u003e\n\u003ctd align=\"right\"\u003e82\u003c/td\u003e\n\u003ctd align=\"right\"\u003e82\u003c/td\u003e\n\u003ctd tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e4.3 Prompt Cache Impact\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-43-prompt-cache-impact\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: 4.3 Prompt Cache Impact\" href=\"#43-prompt-cache-impact\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eBecause sqz operates pre-injection (before content enters the context), the message prefix used for prompt caching remains unchanged. This is a critical advantage over post-hoc pruning methods which modify history and invalidate cached prefixes. Empirically, prompt cache hit rates remain at baseline (90%+) with sqz, compared to approximately 85% with history-modifying approaches [5].\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e4.4 Latency\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-44-latency\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: 4.4 Latency\" href=\"#44-latency\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003esqz is implemented as a single Rust binary with zero runtime dependencies. Compression latency is sub-millisecond for typical outputs (measured p99 \u0026lt; 5ms for outputs up to 100KB). The SQLite dedup lookup adds approximately 0.1ms per call. Total overhead is negligible compared to LLM inference latency.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003chr\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e5. Related Work\u003c/h2\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-5-related-work\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: 5. Related Work\" href=\"#5-related-work\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003ePrompt Compression.\u003c/strong\u003e LLMLingua [2] and LLMLingua-2 [3] achieve 2-20× compression by removing non-essential tokens using a small classifier model. These operate on the \u003cem\u003eentire prompt\u003c/em\u003e (system + user + history) and require an additional LLM inference pass. sqz differs by targeting only tool outputs, requiring no LLM call, and operating at the structural rather than token level.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eContext Pruning.\u003c/strong\u003e ACON [6] optimizes compression guidelines for long-horizon agents by analyzing failure trajectories. Dynamic Context Pruning [5] manages conversation history post-hoc. These complement sqz — they handle what's \u003cem\u003ealready in context\u003c/em\u003e, while sqz reduces what \u003cem\u003eenters\u003c/em\u003e context.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eStructural Compression for Code.\u003c/strong\u003e CodeComp [7] demonstrates that attention-only compression discards structurally critical tokens (call sites, branch conditions). sqz's domain-specific formatters address this by understanding output structure rather than relying on statistical importance.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eInformation-Theoretic Perspectives.\u003c/strong\u003e [12] frames compressor-predictor systems through mutual information, showing that larger compressors are more token-efficient. sqz is consistent with this framework — it functions as a deterministic, zero-parameter \"compressor\" that maximizes information per token through structural awareness rather than learned compression.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eStructurally Lossless Trimming.\u003c/strong\u003e [13] introduces a three-pass algorithm that strips mechanical bloat (raw tool outputs, base64 images, metadata) while preserving user messages verbatim, achieving up to 86% reduction. sqz operates at a complementary point — before injection rather than during trimming — but shares the insight that tool outputs are the dominant source of context bloat.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003chr\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e6. Limitations and Future Work\u003c/h2\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-6-limitations-and-future-work\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: 6. Limitations and Future Work\" href=\"#6-limitations-and-future-work\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cul dir=\"auto\"\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eToken counting\u003c/strong\u003e: the compression pipeline counts tokens with real BPE tokenizers (tiktoken \u003ccode\u003eo200k_base\u003c/code\u003e for OpenAI models — exact; \u003ccode\u003ecl100k_base\u003c/code\u003e as a ~5%-variance approximation for Claude and Gemini; chars/4 for unknown local models). The dedup fast path and MCP-side estimates still use the chars/4 heuristic for latency, so aggregate savings figures mix exact and approximate counts.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eJSONC round-trip\u003c/strong\u003e: For tools using JSON-with-comments configs, programmatic merging cannot preserve comments. sqz warns before merging OpenCode configs and refuses to rewrite Zed settings files entirely, printing a copy-paste snippet instead — safe, but it leaves one manual step for those users.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eNo semantic compression\u003c/strong\u003e: sqz does not use an LLM to summarize content. This is by design (zero latency, zero cost, deterministic), but means it cannot capture semantic redundancy across structurally different outputs.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eDedup granularity\u003c/strong\u003e: exact whole-content hashing, line-aligned slice containment (1.8.0), and near-duplicate deltas via MinHash LSH [14] (1.5.0) cover identical, ranged, and lightly-edited re-reads. Overlaps that are not line-aligned, and structural duplicates across different serializations (the same record fetched once as JSON and once as a table), still re-send in full.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eFuture directions include structural dedup across serializations, integration with KV cache compression methods [7], learned formatters that adapt to project-specific output patterns, and an end-to-end evaluation measuring agent task success with and without compression rather than token counts alone.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003chr\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e7. Conclusion\u003c/h2\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-7-conclusion\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: 7. Conclusion\" href=\"#7-conclusion\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003esqz demonstrates that significant token savings (24.7% average, 92% on dedup hits) are achievable through pre-injection structural compression — without model fine-tuning, additional LLM calls, or changes to the agent workflow. By operating at the tool output boundary, sqz is framework-agnostic, cache-friendly, and transparent. The combination of domain-specific formatters, content deduplication, and adaptive pressure-awareness produces a practical system that reduces costs and improves session longevity for real-world agentic coding workflows.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003chr\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eReferences\u003c/h2\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-references\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: References\" href=\"#references\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e[1] T. KB","deleteTooltip":"You must be signed in to make or propose changes","editTooltip":"You must be signed in to make or propose changes","ghDesktopPath":"https://desktop.github.com","isGitLfs":false,"onBranch":true,"shortPath":"dcd9730","siteNavLoginPath":"/login?return_to=https%3A%2F%2Fgithub.com%2Fojuschugh1%2Fsqz%2Fblob%2Fmain%2Fdocs%2Fwhitepaper.md","isCSV":false,"isRichtext":true,"lineInfo":{"truncatedLoc":"245","truncatedSloc":"151"},"mode":"file"},"image":false,"isCodeownersFile":null,"isPlain":false,"isValidLegacyIssueTemplate":false,"isIssueTemplate":false,"isDiscussionTemplate":false,"language":"Markdown","languageID":222,"large":false,"planSupportInfo":{"repoIsFork":null,"repoOwnedByCurrentUser":null,"requestFullPath":"/ojuschugh1/sqz/blob/main/docs/whitepaper.md","showFreeOrgGatedFeatureMessage":null,"showPlanSupportBanner":null,"upgradeDataAttributes":null,"upgradePath":null},"publishBannersInfo":{"dismissActionNoticePath":"/settings/dismiss-notice/publish_action_from_dockerfile","releasePath":"/ojuschugh1/sqz/releases/new?marketplace=true","showPublishActionBanner":false},"rawBlobUrl":"https://github.com/ojuschugh1/sqz/raw/refs/heads/main/docs/whitepaper.md","renderImageOrRaw":false,"shortPath":null,"symbolsEnabled":true,"tabSize":4,"topBannersInfo":{"overridingGlobalFundingFile":false,"globalPreferredFundingPath":null,"showInvalidCitationWarning":false,"citationHelpUrl":"https://docs.github.com/github/creating-cloning-and-archiving-repositories/creating-a-repository-on-github/about-citation-files","actionsOnboardingTip":null},"truncated":false,"viewable":true,"workflowRedirectUrl":null},"copilotInfo":null,"copilotAccessAllowed":false,"copilotSpacesEnabled":false,"modelsAccessAllowed":false,"modelsRepoIntegrationEnabled":false,"isMarketplaceEnabled":true},"codeViewBlobLayoutRoute.StyledBlob":{"rawLines":["# sqz: Pre-Injection Context Compression for Agentic Code Generation","","**Ojus Chugh** (ojuschugh@gmail.com)","","*May 2026 — revised September 2026 to reflect sqz 1.8.0*","","---","","## Abstract","","Large Language Model (LLM) coding agents process tens of thousands of tokens per session through tool outputs — shell commands, file reads, test results, and build logs. These outputs enter the context window raw, consuming budget that could serve reasoning. We present **sqz**, a transparent compression layer that operates *before* tool outputs are injected into context. sqz combines domain-specific structural formatters, content-addressed deduplication, adaptive pressure-aware compression, and entropy-based safety routing to achieve a mean 24.7% reduction on first-pass content and up to 92% savings on repeated reads — without requiring model fine-tuning, prompt modification, or changes to the agent's workflow. Measured across 3,003 real compressions in production agentic sessions, sqz saved 178,442 tokens while maintaining full semantic fidelity on downstream tasks.","","---","","## 1. Introduction","","The emergence of agentic coding tools — Claude Code, Cursor, Codex, Kiro, Gemini CLI — has shifted LLM usage from single-turn question-answering to multi-turn, tool-augmented sessions that can span thousands of interactions. In these sessions, the dominant source of context consumption is not user prompts or model responses, but **tool outputs**: the results of shell commands, file reads, and API calls that accumulate in the context window over time.","","Recent measurements show that average agent session length tripled from under 2,000 tokens in late 2023 to over 5,400 tokens by late 2025, with the bulk of that growth coming from tool results accumulating in context [1]. This creates a triple penalty: higher costs, increased latency from longer prefills, and degraded reasoning quality as attention must span an ever-growing context.","","Prior work on context compression has focused on two regimes: (1) **prompt compression** that removes redundant tokens from instructions before inference [2, 3, 4], and (2) **post-hoc context pruning** that evicts stale conversation history after it enters the window [5, 6]. Both operate on text *already inside* the context. sqz addresses a third, underexplored regime: **pre-injection compression of tool outputs** — reducing token count before content ever enters the LLM's context window.","","This distinction is architecturally significant. Pre-injection compression:","- Preserves prompt cache validity (the message prefix never changes)","- Operates independently of the LLM provider","- Requires no model cooperation or fine-tuning","- Works transparently across all agent frameworks","","---","","## 2. System Architecture","","\u003cp align=\"center\"\u003e","  \u003cimg src=\"../assets/sqz-architecture.png\" alt=\"sqz system architecture\" width=\"700\" /\u003e","  \u003cbr/\u003e","  \u003cem\u003eFigure 1: sqz system architecture — integration surfaces, 8-stage compression pipeline, and supporting subsystems.\u003c/em\u003e","\u003c/p\u003e","","sqz operates as a PreToolUse hook — a command that the agent framework invokes before delivering tool output to the model. The system comprises four integration surfaces (CLI Hook, MCP Server, Browser Extension, IDE Extension) feeding into a unified Rust core (`sqz_engine`) with an 8-stage compression pipeline. Three supporting subsystems handle persistence (SHA-256 cache, SQLite FTS5 store, CTX session graph), intelligence (TOON lossless encoder, AST parser supporting 18 languages, model router), and analytics (USD cost tracking, multi-agent budgets, correction log). The entire system operates with zero telemetry, fully offline, and is air-gap capable.","","The architecture has five logical stages:","","### 2.1 Domain-Specific Structural Formatters","","Rather than treating all text uniformly (as token-level compression methods do), sqz recognizes 90+ CLI output patterns and applies format-aware transformations:","","- **Test output** → failures only (passing tests stripped)","- **Git status** → compact change summary","- **JSON responses** → null-stripped, key-projected, array-collapsed","- **Docker/kubectl** → name/image/status table extraction","- **Build logs** → error-focused summarization","","This exploits the observation from CodeComp [7] that source code and structured outputs have formal semantics governed by structure rather than surface-level co-occurrence. A `cargo test` output of 500 lines can be faithfully represented by 30 lines containing only the failures and their locations.","","### 2.2 Content-Addressed Deduplication","","Agentic sessions exhibit high repetition: the same file is read multiple times, the same command re-run after edits, the same API response fetched across turns. sqz maintains a persistent SHA-256 content cache (surviving across process invocations via SQLite) and replaces repeated content with a 13-token reference marker (`§ref:HASH§`).","","This is analogous to the demand paging concept described in [8], where context content is loaded on-demand rather than kept resident. The key insight is that in coding sessions, **the dominant savings come not from compressing individual outputs harder, but from recognizing that the same content appears repeatedly.**","","A reference is only emitted while the content it points at is plausibly still in the model's context: refs expire after a freshness window (30 minutes of wall-clock time by default) and are invalidated eagerly when the agent framework compacts its history, at which point the content is re-sent in full. As of 1.4.0, entries can also be **pinned** (`sqz pin`), exempting them from the freshness check — useful for stable knowledge-base content (project docs, system prompts) that multiple agents sharing the session store reference across days.","","**Near-duplicate deltas.** A file re-read after a small edit is not byte-identical, so exact hashing misses it. sqz detects near-duplicates of cached entries and emits a line-level delta — only the changed lines, plus a reference to the version the model already has. Since 1.5.0 a session-lifetime MinHash LSH index selects delta candidates, so a file re-read late in a long session still matches its earlier version rather than only the most recent cache entries.","","**Ranged (slice) references, 1.8.0.** A field measurement over one user's real Claude Code sessions (measurement script published [15]; counters reset at each compaction boundary, edit/write confirmations excluded) found that byte-identical repeats were rare (0.3% of result characters; 1 in 542 file reads) but 8.4% of file reads were a *line range* of a file already read in full — `sed -n '41,80p'` after `cat`, or a ranged read tool call. Exact hashing misses these entirely, and near-duplicate matching does too: a 40-line slice of a 500-line file has a Jaccard similarity near 0.1. sqz therefore checks *containment*: when new output is, on line boundaries, a slice of a fresh full read whose served bytes also contain the slice, it emits `§ref:HASH:L41-80§` instead of the lines. The second condition matters — the slice must appear in what was actually delivered to the model, so a ranged reference can never point at lines a lossy stage dropped. Guards: at least 160 bytes and two lines, and the same freshness and compaction rules as whole-content refs. The reference is not a summary; it is a pointer to bytes the model already holds, and `sqz expand 'HASH:L41-80'` recovers exactly those lines on demand.","","Measured empirically: dedup accounts for 92% reduction on repeat reads, compared to 21-58% from first-pass structural compression.","","### 2.3 Adaptive Pressure-Aware Compression","","Inspired by research on adaptive compression rate allocation [9, 10], sqz dynamically adjusts compression intensity based on session pressure — the rate of token injection over a sliding window:","","- **Low pressure** (\u003c50% of estimated budget consumed in 30 minutes): Default mode, preserve maximum detail","- **High pressure** (\u003e80%): Escalate to aggressive compression, accepting higher information loss","- **Critical** (\u003e90%): Maximum compression + proactive dedup","","This implements the core finding from ACC-RAG [10] that static compression rates are suboptimal: early in a session detail preservation matters more, while late in a session token scarcity demands harder compression.","","### 2.4 Entropy-Based Safety Routing","","Not all content should be compressed. Stack traces contain exact line numbers that compression could corrupt. Configuration files with credentials should not be parsed and reformatted. Migration SQL must be preserved byte-exact.","","sqz uses Shannon entropy analysis combined with pattern detection to classify content into risk tiers:","","- **Safe mode** (0% compression): Stack traces, private keys, credentials, database migrations","- **Default mode** (20-40% compression): Normal code, git output, build logs  ","- **Aggressive mode** (40-60% compression): Repetitive logs, verbose JSON, boilerplate","","This addresses the concern raised in [11] that compression methods which rely on generic importance metrics can inadvertently remove functionally critical tokens.","","Version 1.4.0 extended the safety model with three fidelity guarantees derived from field reports:","","- **Byte-faithful file reads.** The MCP file tools (`sqz_read_file`, `sqz_grep`, `sqz_list_dir`) bypass every content-altering pipeline stage — a file read returns its bytes verbatim apart from ANSI stripping. Token savings on this path come exclusively from dedup references on repeat reads, never from dropping content. (Previously, entropy truncation could silently remove below-median-entropy segments from source files.)","- **Refuse-to-guess formatting.** Structural formatters bail out to raw passthrough rather than emit a partial or misclassified summary. The `git status` formatter, for example, parses porcelain output strictly, matches localized long-format section headers against git's own translation files (it/de/fr/es/pt_PT), and passes any unrecognized locale or section through unmodified — a wrong summary is strictly worse than no compression.","- **UTF-8 boundary safety.** All truncation and splitting of user-controlled text rounds to character boundaries, so densely non-ASCII output (Cyrillic, CJK, Arabic, emoji) can never crash the compressor mid-pipeline; the property is enforced by randomized tests over arbitrary strings and cut points.","","### 2.5 N-gram Abbreviation","","For session-level compression beyond dedup, sqz observes recurring multi-token phrases across the session and introduces compact abbreviations. This technique is inspired by BPE (Byte Pair Encoding) applied at the output level rather than the tokenizer level — frequently co-occurring phrases are replaced with shorter representations.","","Abbreviation is deliberately **lossy for identifiers**: only the first occurrence of a repeated phrase survives verbatim, so a commit SHA or file path embedded in a repeated phrase would be replaced by its abbreviation symbol on later lines. Since 1.4.0 the technique is opt-out (`--no-abbrev` or `SQZ_NO_ABBREV=1`, mirroring the dedup opt-out) and the generated agent guidance documents the escape hatch, so an agent that needs to copy-paste identifiers verbatim can disable it per command.","","---","","## 3. Integration Model","","Unlike prompt compression tools that require API interception or model-specific adapters, sqz integrates at the **tool execution boundary** — a universal hook point that exists in every agent framework:","","| Framework | Hook Mechanism |","|-----------|---------------|","| Claude Code | PreToolUse JSON hook (Bash and PowerShell tools) |","| Cursor | .cursor/rules/*.mdc |","| Windsurf | .windsurfrules guidance |","| Cline / Roo Code | .clinerules guidance |","| Kiro | .kiro/hooks/ JSON schema |","| Gemini CLI | BeforeTool hook |","| OpenCode | TypeScript plugin |","| Codex | AGENTS.md guidance + config.toml MCP entry |","| Zed | .rules/AGENTS.md instructions + context_servers MCP entry |","| Copilot CLI | preToolUse hook |","| Any MCP server | `sqz-mcp proxy` wrapper (since 1.6.0) |","","A single `sqz init` command detects installed frameworks and configures the appropriate hooks. The compression is transparent — the agent receives compressed output without knowing sqz exists.","","Since 1.6.0 the MCP proxy provides a framework-independent integration surface: `sqz-mcp proxy -- \u003cupstream command\u003e` wraps any stdio MCP server, compresses its tool results through the full pipeline (dedup references on repeats, safe mode for stack traces and secrets, error results untouched), optionally compacts verbose tool descriptions in `tools/list`, and injects an `sqz_expand` tool so the agent can recover any original byte-exact. The client sees a normal MCP server, so this works with every MCP client regardless of whether it supports hooks.","","---","","## 4. Evaluation","","### 4.1 Token Savings","","Measured across a real developer's week of agentic coding (3,003 compressions):","","| Metric | Value |","|--------|-------|","| Total compressions | 3,003 |","| Tokens in (original) | 721,840 |","| Tokens out (compressed) | 543,398 |","| **Tokens saved** | **178,442** |","| Average reduction | 24.7% |","| Peak reduction (dedup) | 92% |","","### 4.2 Per-Category Performance","","| Content Type | Before | After | Reduction |","|---|---:|---:|---:|","| Repeated file reads (5×) | 10,000 | 826 | 92% |","| Test-fix-test cycle (3 runs) | 15,000 | 5,186 | 65% |","| Repeated JSON response (3×) | 192 | 79 | 59% |","| Repeated log lines | 148 | 62 | 58% |","| Large JSON array | 259 | 142 | 45% |","| Git diff | 61 | 54 | 12% |","| Stack trace (safe mode) | 82 | 82 | 0% |","","### 4.3 Prompt Cache Impact","","Because sqz operates pre-injection (before content enters the context), the message prefix used for prompt caching remains unchanged. This is a critical advantage over post-hoc pruning methods which modify history and invalidate cached prefixes. Empirically, prompt cache hit rates remain at baseline (90%+) with sqz, compared to approximately 85% with history-modifying approaches [5].","","### 4.4 Latency","","sqz is implemented as a single Rust binary with zero runtime dependencies. Compression latency is sub-millisecond for typical outputs (measured p99 \u003c 5ms for outputs up to 100KB). The SQLite dedup lookup adds approximately 0.1ms per call. Total overhead is negligible compared to LLM inference latency.","","---","","## 5. Related Work","","**Prompt Compression.** LLMLingua [2] and LLMLingua-2 [3] achieve 2-20× compression by removing non-essential tokens using a small classifier model. These operate on the *entire prompt* (system + user + history) and require an additional LLM inference pass. sqz differs by targeting only tool outputs, requiring no LLM call, and operating at the structural rather than token level.","","**Context Pruning.** ACON [6] optimizes compression guidelines for long-horizon agents by analyzing failure trajectories. Dynamic Context Pruning [5] manages conversation history post-hoc. These complement sqz — they handle what's *already in context*, while sqz reduces what *enters* context.","","**Structural Compression for Code.** CodeComp [7] demonstrates that attention-only compression discards structurally critical tokens (call sites, branch conditions). sqz's domain-specific formatters address this by understanding output structure rather than relying on statistical importance.","","**Information-Theoretic Perspectives.** [12] frames compressor-predictor systems through mutual information, showing that larger compressors are more token-efficient. sqz is consistent with this framework — it functions as a deterministic, zero-parameter \"compressor\" that maximizes information per token through structural awareness rather than learned compression.","","**Structurally Lossless Trimming.** [13] introduces a three-pass algorithm that strips mechanical bloat (raw tool outputs, base64 images, metadata) while preserving user messages verbatim, achieving up to 86% reduction. sqz operates at a complementary point — before injection rather than during trimming — but shares the insight that tool outputs are the dominant source of context bloat.","","---","","## 6. Limitations and Future Work","","- **Token counting**: the compression pipeline counts tokens with real BPE tokenizers (tiktoken `o200k_base` for OpenAI models — exact; `cl100k_base` as a ~5%-variance approximation for Claude and Gemini; chars/4 for unknown local models). The dedup fast path and MCP-side estimates still use the chars/4 heuristic for latency, so aggregate savings figures mix exact and approximate counts.","- **JSONC round-trip**: For tools using JSON-with-comments configs, programmatic merging cannot preserve comments. sqz warns before merging OpenCode configs and refuses to rewrite Zed settings files entirely, printing a copy-paste snippet instead — safe, but it leaves one manual step for those users.","- **No semantic compression**: sqz does not use an LLM to summarize content. This is by design (zero latency, zero cost, deterministic), but means it cannot capture semantic redundancy across structurally different outputs.","- **Dedup granularity**: exact whole-content hashing, line-aligned slice containment (1.8.0), and near-duplicate deltas via MinHash LSH [14] (1.5.0) cover identical, ranged, and lightly-edited re-reads. Overlaps that are not line-aligned, and structural duplicates across different serializations (the same record fetched once as JSON and once as a table), still re-send in full.","","Future directions include structural dedup across serializations, integration with KV cache compression methods [7], learned formatters that adapt to project-specific output patterns, and an end-to-end evaluation measuring agent task success with and without compression rather than token counts alone.","","---","","## 7. Conclusion","","sqz demonstrates that significant token savings (24.7% average, 92% on dedup hits) are achievable through pre-injection structural compression — without model fine-tuning, additional LLM calls, or changes to the agent workflow. By operating at the tool output boundary, sqz is framework-agnostic, cache-friendly, and transparent. The combination of domain-specific formatters, content deduplication, and adaptive pressure-awareness produces a practical system that reduces costs and improves session longevity for real-world agentic coding workflows.","","---","","## References","","[1] T. Pan, \"Tool Output Compression: The Injection Decision That Shapes Context Quality,\" 2026. Available: https://tianpan.co/blog/2026-04-20-tool-output-compression-context-injection","","[2] H. Jiang, Q. Wu, C.-Y. Lin, Y. Yang, and L. Qiu, \"LLMLingua: Compressing Prompts for Accelerated Inference of Large Language Models,\" in *Proc. EMNLP*, 2023. arXiv:2310.05736","","[3] Z.
